L2755: ## Effects of taletrectinib on OATP1B and BCRP substrate rosuvastatin
L2756: The PBPK analysis cannot rule out a potential inhibition effect of taletrectinib on BCRP substrates for reasons discussed below:
L2757: As for the OATP1B inhibition parameters, the in vitro to in vivo extrapolation of BCRP inhibition parameter (Ki or IC50) has not been established. Based on the data from BCRP inhibitors that have observed interaction data with both OATP1B1 endogenous substrates and rosuvastatin, the reviewer's independent analysis indicated that the estimated in vivo Ki values for BCRP, using the Costales' rosuvastatin model, were approximately 3- to 50-fold lower compared to the corresponding lowest IC50 values of these inhibitors in the Certara DDI and FDA Databases (data not shown). Taletrectinib was predicted to increase rosuvastatin AUC and Cmax significantly when simulations were carried out with in vitro IC50 values of OATP1B and BCRP reduced simultaneously (Table 97). It should also be noted that the lowest IC50 values of BCRP inhibitors in the DDI Database were mostly generated from the membrane vesicle system, not the Caco-2 monolayer system used for taletrectinib. The BCRP IC50 generated using the Caco-2 monolayer system was on average 11-fold less potent, ranging from 0.3 to 53-fold less potent, compared to the HEK293 membrane vesicle system. Furthermore, taletrectinib had low to moderate permeability across the Caco-2 cell monolayer (Report No. 423090-20210624-Caco2). Given inhibition occurs at the intracellular domain of BCRP molecules, the actual IC50 value may be lower than that estimated based on the nominal concentrations in the BCRP inhibition study. Taken together, these findings suggest that a significant interaction between taletrectinib and rosuvastatin cannot be ruled out.
L2758: ## Effects of taletrectinib on MATE1 substrate Metformin
L2759: The ability of metformin model to predict MATE1 inhibition has not been well verified because the in vitro to in vivo extrapolation of MATEs/OCTs inhibition parameter K_i value has not been established. Three inhibitors of MATEs cimetidine, pyrimethamine and trimethoprim were reported to increase metformin exposure, and data from clinical DDI studies of metformin with these inhibitors were used to verify the metformin model (Simcyp metformin summary report). Although the simulated effects of these inhibitors on metformin exposure were within 0.78 to 1.41-fold of the observed effects, the K_i value of MATEs in the cimetidine model was 3- to 15-fold lower than its IC50 values of MATE1 in the Certara DDI database and 8- to 33-fold lower than its IC50 values of MATE-2K published in the database. For OCT2, the in vitro K_i values reported in the database were equal to or 12-fold lower than that in the cimetidine model, approximately 4-fold lower than that in the pyrimethamine model, and 2-fold higher than that in the trimethoprim model. These differences between in vitro K_i and model used K_i suggest that, without the results from the clinical DDI studies, the effects of these inhibitors on metformin exposure cannot be confidently predicted. It should be noted that, if metformin efficacy is of concern, evaluating metformin plasma exposure alone may be insufficient to inform the metformin dosing (PMID: 29761830). Overall, the PBPK analysis of the interaction of taletrectinib with metformin is considered inadequate.

L2762: Refer to **Section 8.2** for FDA's safety analyses.

L2774: Line graph showing Estimated tumor volume (mm^3) versus Days after the first administration (0 to 4) for Vehicle control, DS-6051b (3, 10, 30, 100 mg/kg), and Crizotinib (100 mg/kg) groups. The Vehicle control and Crizotinib groups show rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 450 mm^3 by Day 4. The DS-6051b 100 mg/kg group shows the strongest inhibition, with tumor volume decreasing significantly to around 35 mm^3 by Day 4. DS-6051b 30 mg/kg also shows substantial inhibition, reaching around 150 mm^3 by Day 4. DS-6051b 3 mg/kg and 10 mg/kg show moderate inhibition, reaching around 300 mm^3 and 350 mm^3, respectively, by Day 4.
L2775: Figure 3 is a Kaplan-Meier survival curve illustrating the effects of taletrectinib (AHT-2101-H) on the survival time of LU-01-0414 tumor-bearing BALB/C Nude Mice. The X-axis represents Days after the start of treatment (0 to 80), and the Y-axis represents Percent survival (0 to 100).
The legend indicates three groups:
Vehicle (Black line): Shows rapid decline in survival, reaching 0% survival around day 25.
AHT-2101-H, 30 mg/kg (Orange line): Shows intermediate survival, reaching 0% survival around day 45.
AHT-2101-H, 100 mg/kg (Red line): Shows the highest survival rate, reaching approximately 80% survival at day 70, and remaining stable thereafter.
The data demonstrates a dose-dependent survival benefit with taletrectinib treatment compared to the vehicle control.
L2776: Western blot analysis showing the effects of Taletrectinib (DS-6051b) on ROS1 phosphorylation and downstream signaling in Ba/F3-CD74-ROS1 cells. The blot displays results for various concentrations of DS-6051b (0.1 nM, 1 nM, 10 nM, 100 nM, 1000 nM) compared to DMSO control. The analyzed proteins include Phospho-ROS1, ROS1, Phospho-SHP-2, SHP-2, Phospho-MEK1, MEK1, Phospho-ERK1/2, ERK1/2, Phospho-AKT, AKT, and Actin (loading control). The results demonstrate that increasing concentrations of DS-6051b lead to a dose-dependent decrease in the levels of phosphorylated ROS1, SHP-2, MEK1, ERK1/2, and AKT, while total protein levels remain relatively stable.
L2777: Western blot analysis showing inhibition of ROS1 phosphorylation in tumor tissue from mice bearing Ba/F3-CD74-ROS1 (Left) and Ba/F3-CD74-ROS1^G2032R (Right) gene-driven tumors. The blots are arranged in columns corresponding to Vehicle, DS-6051b (Taletrectinib) at doses of 3, 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg, and time points of 2, 6, and 24 hours post-dose. The rows show detection of Phospho-Ros1, Ros1, and Actin. Inhibition of Phospho-Ros1 is dose- and time-dependent, with the highest inhibition observed at 100 mg/kg and 24 hours.
L2778: Line graph showing Mean Tumor Volume (mm^3) versus Day (0 to 30) for ROS1 Gene Fusion Human NSCLC Tumor Bearing Mice treated daily for 28 days. The Vehicle Control PO QD group shows tumor growth up to approximately 550 mm^3. The DS-6051b (taletrectinib) groups (10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg, and 100 mg/kg PO QD) show dose-dependent inhibition of tumor growth, with the 100 mg/kg group showing the greatest inhibition, stabilizing around 50 mm^3.

L2792: Key eligibility criteria:
Locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC or other solid tumors
Evidence of ROS1 fusion-positive tumor by CLIA validated test
At least 1 measurable lesion per RECIST 1.1
ECOG PS 0 or 1
Adequate organ function
Key study endpoints:
Primary endpoint: cORR assessed by RECISTv1.1 by IRC
Secondary endpoints: DOR, DCR, PFS, OS, IC-ORR
* Prior to PA 3.0, \le 2 TKI with ROS1 activity; \le 2 Chemo

L2803: Observed vs Predicted (Population/Individual)
Model: e524 (All Clean Data)
The figure displays six Goodness-of-Fit plots arranged in a 3x2 grid, comparing Observed Concentration [ng/mL] (Y-axis) against Predicted Concentration [ng/mL] (X-axis). A red diagonal line represents perfect agreement (Observed = Predicted).
The plots are organized by population/individual status and disease type:
Row 1: Population (Left) and Individual (Right)
Row 2: Healthy (Left) and NSCLC (Right)

L2819: Figure 37 displays three Visual Predictive Check (VPC) plots comparing the Objective Response Rate (Y-axis) against different covariates (X-axis) for the Updated E-R Model of ORR. The observed data is represented by the gray bars, and the predicted median is represented by the black dots with 90% prediction intervals (error bars).
The plots are:
By Cavgss Quartile (ng/mL): Shows four quartiles of Cavgss (148–392, 392–500, 500–609, 609–1.43e+03). The Objective Response Rate is consistently high, around 0.6, across all quartiles.
By Albumin Quartile (g/L): Shows four quartiles of Albumin (24–36, 36–40, 40–43, 43–54). The Objective Response Rate increases from approximately 0.45 in the lowest quartile to approximately 0.75 in the highest quartile.
By Prior TKI Treatment: Compares TKI (Prior TKI Treatment) and Naive groups. The Objective Response Rate is approximately 0.45 for TKI and approximately 0.75 for Naive.
